先週（10/26～10/30）発表の主な経済指標
AI関連を中心に設備投資が増勢を維持し、産業用機械にも広
がり。好調な企業収益も投資を後押し。個人消費も堅調。金
融所得や移転所得、株高による資産効果が購買力を下支え
政策要因を除く都区部CPIは上昇基調。財価格の上昇が目立
つ一方、サービス価格改定の広がりは限定的。企業の価格転嫁
は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが値上げの重荷に

のコアPCE物価は3％を超える伸びが継続。景気の

労働分配率は低下傾向。賃金
以外の所得が家計所得を補完
米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完
― 株式配当や社会保障給付など、賃金以外の所得が家計の購買力を支える構図
 こうした所得環境に加え、株高による資産効果も消費を下支え。10月入り後も個人消費は底堅く推移
― 燃料高にもかかわらず、空港利用者数は前年並みの水準を維持し、小売売上高も高めの伸びを維持
― 年末商戦も前年を上回る伸びが予想されている

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大
（注） 食品は、コメと外食を除く食料価格。水道料金はその他財に該当。サービスは一般
サービスと公共サービスの合計（家賃を除く）

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金
融政策は有効
 信用スプレッドの拡大などは「市場参加
者が、どのような投資を行いたいか、資
本配分を選別している」ことの表れ
AI投資
 「経済全体の生産性は、ここ2、3年ほど
上向いて」おり、高い経済成⾧率によっ
てインフレ圧力が生まれ、利上げが必要
な状況ではないとの認識
生産性
11月16日に生産性・雇用の公聴会。タスク
フォース報告は年末が目安（一部は遅延の
可能性）。バランスシート提言は2027年1
～3月期の早期を想定

### Page 8

内容ポイント
 26年3Qの景気は引き続き拡大
 エネルギー価格と資金調達コストの上昇が今後の重しに景気
 エネルギー価格の見通しは9月の予想を上回っている
 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ
 物価の基調・賃金はおおむね安定。ただし、価格形成の初
期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく
物価
 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、
複合的な要因が金利を押し上げ。加えて、債務の多い加盟
国は固有の課題に直面
 市場の不当かつ無秩序な動向が金融政策伝達の妨げとな
る場合、対応手段は用意されている
金融
環境
 全会一致で金利据え置きも、利上げが適切かどうか議論し
た。「今利上げを実施してはどうか」との意見もあった
 今後の金融政策はデータに基づき、会合ごとに決定

（「増加」－「減少」、％）
欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
金利水準の高さが
貸出需要を下押し

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVAT免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

中国：製造業利益は引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引。7～9月期の利益率は前期を下回る
 9月の製造業総利益は前年比＋14.4％と前月（同＋9.1％）から加速。引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引
― コンピューター・通信機器は同＋72.9％の増益。石油・石炭加工は単月赤字から一転し、同10倍超の伸び
― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

― 韓国、台湾の主要な半導体・電子部品企業は、生産能力増強のため大規模な設備増強投資を計画

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速

― タイでは燃料を中心とする交通費、フィリピンでは交通関連に加え、宿泊飲食なども抑制する形で消費者が節約行動
 背景に実質賃金の鈍化による購買力の低下
― 購買力低下を受け、タイは6月～11月に消費支援策（食品・日用品などの購入補助）を実施、フィリピンは政府による企
業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

（2026年10月2日発刊）

世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

多重利空夾擊，全球 AI 股連日重挫
2026 年 11 月 20 日
一、市場動態
1. 美股 11/19 反彈遭逢賣壓：Anthropic 19 日於那斯達克掛牌，募資約 813 億美元創史
上最大 IPO，開盤大漲 34%、盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，終場漲幅收斂至 17.1%，
然激勵效果未能擴散至其他 AI 股，反被視為「利多出盡」的獲利了結訊號。美股四大
指數開高走低、全數重挫逾 2%：道瓊下跌 2.19%，為 6 月以來首度跌破 50,000 點；
標普 500 跌 2.02%、連六黑，逾 460 檔成分股收黑；那斯達克跌 2.25%、費半跌 2.41%，

VIX 盤中一度升近 29、創 3 月以來新高。個股方面，輝達早盤一度上漲 4.7%後翻黑，
收跌 3.3%，本月累計跌幅約 28%；AMD 遭放空機構 Hunterbrook 指控與受美國制裁
的中國伺服器業者往來，重挫 7.72%，高度依賴舉債擴張的 CoreWeave、Nebius 分
別重挫 6.1%與 9.4%。Fed 官員密集釋出鷹派訊號，警告 AI 建設熱潮恐推升通膨，市
場預估 12 月升息機率約 96%；初領失業金人數亦高於預期，增添景氣疑慮。
2. 亞股 11/20 續挫，台股同步承壓：亞股 11/20 早盤延續美股走勢，賣壓擴及亞洲 AI
硬體供應鏈，日經 225 與韓國 KOSPI 同步重挫逾 4%。台股 19 日下跌 1,768.02 點，
收 38,426.89 點，跌幅 4.40%，創史上收盤第 7 大跌點後，20 日再受美股下挫拖累，
加權指數持續走低，截至台灣時間下午 1 點 22 分為止，台股加權指數下挫 6.80%；台
積電、聯發科、台達電等權值股同步重挫。

二、下跌原因
1. 美國對中 AI 管制全面升級，從「晶片」延伸到「算力」與「代工」：美國商務部工業暨
安全局（BIS）於 11 月 16 日發布臨時最終規則、並於 19 日生效，將管制由實體晶片

擴大至「提供雲端算力給中國客戶的第三國資料中心」，並將印尼、馬來西亞、新加坡、
泰國及巴西列為高轉運風險國家，同時限縮晶圓代工與封測廠的許可豁免。此次新規
由財政部與白宮國安會主導起草，反映黃仁勳辭任後政策方向明顯從嚴。對台股而言，

者認為未來數月可能需要再升息，理事華勒更表示 12 月可考慮升息 2 碼；副主席傑佛
森與芝加哥 Fed 總裁古爾斯比亦示警 AI 建設熱潮正推升需求與通膨。依 CME 
FedWatch 約台灣時間上午 11 時的數據，市場預估 12 月升息 1 碼機率約 96%，美國
10 年期公債殖利率一度升至 5.12%。利率上升同時墊高資料中心融資成本、拉長投資

3. 輝達「利多不漲」且接班未定，市場由交易成長轉為交易風險：輝達 FY2027 第三季營
收與下季指引均優於預期，Vera Rubin 亦已量產出貨，但調整後毛利率指引下滑至 71%
～72%；董事會已表明黃仁勳不回任，並委託獵才公司物色新任執行長。財報利多仍無
法止跌，顯示市場對 AI 龍頭的定價重心，已由成長性轉向治理、政策與獲利率風險。
表三：輝達 FY2027 第三季財報重點
項目 數值 市場解讀

4. Anthropic 史上最大 IPO 引發資金排擠與「利多出盡」情緒：Anthropic 募資約 813
億美元，盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，但美股開高走低、晶片股由紅翻黑，市場將其視
為 AI 交易階段性高點的獲利了結訊號。市場亦解讀，大型 AI 模型公司直接上市，讓資
金可直接押注 AI 應用端，相對降低透過硬體供應鏈間接參與的需求，並在短期內吸納
部分 AI 配置資金。
5. 台股籌碼面賣壓與融資斷頭形成負向循環：外資 11 月累計賣超達 8,303 億元，台指期
淨空單維持約 9.9 萬口；台股跌破年線後融資維持率偏低，斷頭與停損賣壓同步出籠，
新台幣亦貶至 33 元附近，加重外資匯損考量。金管會雖自 20 日起實施限空令 2.0（個

股收跌逾 3.5%者隔日禁止平盤下放空、盤中借券賣出上限由 3%降至 1%），但短線仍
難完全抵銷被動賣壓。
資料來源：臺灣證券交易所、元大銀行整理
三、市場看法
1. AI 需求尚未反轉，但產業正由資本支出驅動轉向獲利驗證：我們認為，判斷 AI 產業是
否出現結構性反轉，關鍵應在於終端運算需求、CSP 資本支出及供應鏈訂單是否持續下

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
降，而非單純觀察股價跌幅。輝達第三季資料中心營收年增 110%，且 Vera Rubin 平
台已進入出貨階段，顯示先進運算設備需求仍具有實質支撐。此外，Reuters 引述 LSEG
資料指出，輝達財報公布後，分析師對下一財年調整後淨利的平均預估由 3,800 億美元
上修至 3,930 億美元，但股價評價反而轉趨保守。這項變化反映市場並未全面否定輝達
的獲利能力，而是開始要求更高的風險補償。我們認為，若未來 CSP 未大幅縮減資本
支出，AI 晶片與相關供應鏈仍具有中長期成長基礎；但若資本支出成長持續快於營收

及現金流改善，科技股評價仍可能承壓。
2. AI 信用風險仍較集中於高槓桿業者，尚未顯示全面性信用危機：目前市場對 AI 企業融
資風險的擔憂逐漸升高，但不同企業的財務承受能力存在明顯差異。大型 CSP 通常擁
有成熟的雲端、廣告或軟體業務，可透過營業現金流支應部分 AI 投資；相較之下，部
分新興資料中心業者高度依賴債務、設備融資或外部股權資金，對利率與資本市場環境
更加敏感。10 月 FOMC 會議紀錄指出，投資級公司債利差僅小幅擴大，且以歷史標準
衡量仍相對偏低；高收益債利差則擴大較明顯，尤其與資料中心融資有關的債券受到更
大衝擊。這顯示市場正在提高部分高風險企業的融資門檻，但尚不足以判定信用壓力已
全面擴散至整體投資級債市場。我們認為，後續仍應觀察相關企業的債務到期結構、再
融資需求與信用利差變化，判斷風險是否進一步擴大。
3. 科技股次產業將進一步分化：隨著市場由追逐 AI 投資規模轉向評估投資效益，科技次
產業未來表現可能明顯分歧。晶圓代工與先進封裝產業具有較高技術門檻，仍可受惠先

進運算晶片需求；HBM 及高階記憶體則具備 AI 伺服器成長題材，但其獲利仍需觀察供
需循環及價格變化；半導體設備業者則較容易受到客戶建廠時程及資本支出調整影響。
另一方面，部分尚未建立穩定營收模式的 AI 基礎建設業者，可能因融資成本與投資回
收壓力，面臨更大的評價修正。我們認為，科技股中期並非全面轉空，而是市場可能更
偏好具備技術競爭優勢、穩定訂單、獲利成長及自由現金流的企業。未來即使 AI 產業
整體維持成長，也不代表所有相關公司的營收、獲利及股價都能同步受惠。
4. Fed 政策仍有不確定性，但企業獲利成長可望部分抵銷評價壓力：目前 Fed 升息預期
已對全球科技股評價形成明顯壓力，但未來市場方向不僅取決於 12 月是否升息，更重
要的是升息幅度與後續政策路徑是否超出市場預期。然而，美國經濟與企業獲利目前尚
未出現全面性衰退跡象，若未來企業營收與獲利持續成長，即使本益比難以回到過去高
檔，仍有助於股市透過基本面改善逐步修復跌幅。我們認為，Fed 政策風險不容忽視，
但也不宜單憑升息預期就認定股市中長期多頭結構已全面改變。

5. 台股短線仍需消化籌碼壓力，但企業獲利成長仍可提供中期支撐：若以台股走勢來看，
其近期跌幅明顯超過部分國際市場，我們認為除受到美股科技股修正影響，也與大型電
子權值股集中、外資調節及短線市場流動性下降有關。不過，根據金管會公布數據，截
至 2026 年 10 月底，國內上市櫃公司累計營收約 56.75 兆元，年增 39.05%；前三季
累計稅前淨利約 6.61 兆元，年增 86.43%，顯示整體企業獲利仍具成長支撐。此外，
金管會自 11 月 20 日起擴大限空措施，對收盤跌幅逾 3.5%的個股實施次日平盤下放空
限制，並將盤中借券賣出數量上限由 3%降至 1%。我們認為，政策措施有助降低部分

### Page 5

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
短線賣壓，但市場真正止穩仍需觀察外資流向、融資去槓桿是否趨緩，以及主要電子權
值股的股價與基本面是否恢復穩定。
6. 後續觀察重點：（1）美國下週公布核心 PCE 物價指數，市場預期年增 3.2%，將影響
12 月升息幅度；（2）12 月 FOMC 升息 1 碼或 2 碼，以及 2027 年升息路徑；（3）輝
達新任執行長人選與黃仁勳赴參議院作證情況，牽動出口管制後續走向；（4）BIS 新規
執行細節及東南亞資料中心許可審查進度。
四、 投資建議
1. 短線嚴控槓桿與部位集中度，選股邏輯轉向「現金流與訂單能見度」優先：我們認為，

AI 產業成長動能尚未確認反轉，但市場已不再接受單純依靠資本支出擴張支撐評價。
前述利空因素短期內恐難完全消除或明朗，市場短線仍將隨著消息面的發展而變化。我
們預期後續投資人將更重視科技股的訂單能見度、企業獲利、資產負債表及自由現金
流。同時，台灣在先進製程、先進封裝及 AI 伺服器領域仍具競爭優勢，中期基本面展
望維持偏正向。然而，近期指數連續重挫，反映市場流動性與籌碼壓力尚未解除，短期
仍須觀察外資賣壓、融資水位及美股科技股走勢。投資人宜採取分批及分散方式參與中
長期產業成長，同時避免過度集中於融資需求高、獲利能見度較低的 AI 企業。
2. 以股債平衡穿越波動，固定收益「先短後長」：Fed 仍處升息循環，長天期債券價格仍
有波動風險，但美國公債目前具有高殖利率吸引力與防禦價值，建議以中短天期美國公
債、高信評投資等級債及美元貨幣型商品鎖定相對高檔的收益，待升息循環接近尾聲再
逐步拉長存續期間。投資級債則受惠相對穩健的信用品質，惟仍須關注 AI 相關企業信
用風險是否進一步擴散。

主因政府支出成長高於收入。9 月單月由去年同期盈餘轉為 269 億美元

8.4%。在聯邦債務總額逾 40 兆美元下，財政赤字擴大及債務融資成本
攀升，持續考驗財政永續性。

升至 2.6%。受能源價格上揚影響，能源指數月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格

美國通膨壓力較前月升溫，核心物價漲勢亦略有增強。 
⚫ 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 1.3%，年增率由 5.6%升至 6.3%；能

亦月增 0.8%，核心 PPI 年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%，顯示生產端通膨壓力
持續升高。

一、 美國—內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
2026 年 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,450 億美元，較
上月增加 0.8%，年增 6.3%。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.9%；進一步
扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增 0.2%，顯示整體消費需求仍維持
成長。從主要業別表現觀察，加油站、非實體零售商及餐飲服務與飲酒場所銷售
額均較上月增加，其中加油站及餐飲消費表現相對亮眼，電子及家電商店銷售亦
呈成長。整體而言，9 月美國零售銷售延續擴張走勢，顯示民間消費仍具支撐力
道，且多數主要消費類別維持正成長，惟核心零售增幅較前月放緩，後續仍須關
注物價、就業及金融情勢變化對消費支出的影響。 
2026 年 9 月(2026 財年第 12 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字為 269.0 億美元，較
去年同期 1,979.4 億美元財政盈餘轉為赤字。當月政府收入 5,578.8 億美元，年增
2.6%；政府支出 5,847.8 億美元，年增 69.1%，支出增幅明顯高於收入。收入方
面，9 月關稅淨收入由 8 月的 128.4 億美元降至 59.1 億美元，累計 2026 財年關稅
淨收入約 1,732.2 億美元，年減 11.1%，關稅退稅增加已部分抵銷關稅收入成長。
支出方面，9 月聯邦政府淨利息支出約 347.9 億美元，全年累計達 1 兆 517.5 億美
元，年增 8.4%，反映高利率環境下債務利息負擔持續攀升。累計 2026 財年全年，
美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，較 2025 財年擴大 12.2%。其中，
政府收入年增 3.2%至 5 兆 4,033.3 億美元，支出年增 5.5%至 7 兆 3,958.0 億美元，
顯示支出成長仍快於收入增加。從支出結構觀察，社會安全、醫療保險、國防及
淨利息支出均較上年增加，持續推升財政壓力。整體而言，美國財政赤字擴大主
要反映政府支出成長強勁、利息負擔居高不下，以及關稅退稅增加削弱部分收入
效果。在聯邦債務總額突破 40 兆美元背景下，未來財政赤字走勢、債務融資成本
及財政永續性問題，仍將是市場關注的重要焦點。 
2026 年 9 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率升至 399 萬戶，較上月增加 1.5%，
惟較去年同期減少 2.2%，整體房市成交動能仍偏疲弱。區域表現方面，南部及西
部分別月增 2.8%，中西部持平，東北部月減 2.1%，四大地區銷售量均較去年同
期下滑。房價方面，成屋售價中位數為 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，已連續 39 個

月維持年增走勢。供給端則持續增加，待售成屋庫存升至 160 萬戶，較上月增加
1.9%，較去年同期增加 4.6%，相當於 4.8 個月供給量，高於去年同期的 4.5 個月。
全美住房可負擔指數由去年同期 106.7 降至 105.4，顯示購屋負擔仍受房價及利率
影響。整體而言，9 月美國成屋市場呈現成交量略有回升、房價維持溫和漲勢及
待售庫存持續累積的格局，顯示供給條件改善，但高利率環境仍對購屋需求形成
壓力。 
2026 年 9 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於上月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；扣除食品與能源之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與上月
持平，年增率則由 2.4%升至 2.6%。在細項方面，能源價格持續上揚，能源指數
月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格大幅上漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅逾半，為本月通
膨升溫主因；住房指數月增 0.4%，住房項目價格漲幅尚未明顯趨緩。食品指數則
持平，其中外食價格月增 0.2%，家庭食品價格月減 0.1%。此外，二手車及卡車、
機票、休閒娛樂及服飾等價格上漲，通訊價格則下跌。整體而言，9 月美國通膨
明顯回升，主要受汽油價格大幅上漲及住房成本續增帶動；核心 CPI 月增率維持
0.3%，年增率則回升至 2.6%，顯示核心物價壓力較前月略有增強，未來通膨走勢
仍須持續關注能源價格變化及住房等服務項目價格表現。 
 2026 年 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 1.3%，高於上月之
0.5%，年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 2.6%，主要受
能源價格月增 10.8%帶動，以汽油、柴油及航空燃油價格漲幅較為顯著；食品價
格亦月增 1.2%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.8%，主要反映批發及零售
利潤率、運輸服務價格上升。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求 PPI
月增 0.5%，高於上月之 0.3%，年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%。整體而言，9 月 PPI 升
幅明顯擴大，主要反映能源與服務價格同步上揚，顯示生產端成本壓力仍偏高，
後續仍須關注能源價格及運輸成本變化對通膨走勢之影響。 
10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員針對貨幣政策發表看法。舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴
利(Mary Daly)表示，雖然通膨仍高於 2%目標，未來可能仍需進一步升息，但目
前無迫切必要在 10 月 27 日至 28 日 FOMC 會議立即採取行動，顯示聯準會傾向
先觀察後續數據表現。克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth Hammack)則警告，若
通膨預期持續惡化，聯準會可能必須採取更積極的緊縮措施，以維持物價穩定。
聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)10 月 16 日則表示，美國經濟受穩健就業市場及 AI

投資熱潮支撐而維持韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力，並
未對下次利率決策預作表態。整體而言，聯準會官員發言顯示政策立場仍偏鷹派，
但對短期升息時點保持彈性，未來通膨及通膨預期變化仍是利率決策的重要依據。 
近期美墨貿易談判再度受到關稅爭議影響。據《華爾街日報》報導，雙方原
已接近達成初步協議，美國擬調降對墨西哥汽車、鋼鐵及鋁製品的部分進口關稅，
以換取墨西哥在貿易政策、商品採購及其他經貿議題上的讓步，並為後續《美墨
加協定》（USMCA）檢討與延長協商創造有利條件。然而，美國商務部長 Howard 
Lutnick 反對大幅調降目前對墨西哥汽車課徵的 25%關稅，以及鋼鐵、鋁製品與部
分衍生製成品的 50%關稅，使談判進展受阻。墨西哥方面則希望將汽車關稅降至
15%或以下，以縮小與日本、南韓汽車適用稅率的差距，並擴大汽車關稅抵減機
制對美墨加三國零組件價值的認列範圍；惟美方仍須考量國內金屬產業對降稅可
能衝擊本土生產及就業的疑慮。儘管雙方對關稅調整幅度仍存分歧，墨西哥經貿
官員表示協商已有部分進展，美國商務部亦強調持續與墨方保持溝通，顯示談判
尚未正式破局。整體而言，美墨關稅協商結果不僅攸關北美汽車及金屬產業供應
鏈成本與競爭力，也可能影響 USMCA 後續檢討與延長協商進程；若雙方遲遲無
法縮小分歧，恐增加企業投資與供應鏈布局的不確定性，並對北美區域貿易整合
形成壓力。 
二、歐元區—工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%，呈現連續三個月下降，
其中非耐久消費財、中間財及能源產出分別減少 2.7%、0.8%、0.6%，僅資本財、
耐久消費財產出分別成長 0.4%、0.2%。儘管德國 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)反彈
0.4%，係受惠車廠陸續結束歲修，但法、義、西工業生產分別下跌 0.3%、1.3%、
0.8%為主要拖累因素，反映先前美伊戰爭帶來的庫存儲備需求逐漸消退，尤其以
能源密集產業(化工、造紙、鋼鐵等)變化最為明顯。整體而言，即便排除能源密集
產業的波動，其他製造業諸如製藥、汽車、機械等生產情形皆偏疲軟。 
 展望未來，近期德國在財政擴張的支持下，工廠訂單大幅成長，未來可望陸
續反映在該國工業生產表現；但法國政治、選舉等不利前景重挫市場信心，恐會
影響企業以及政府在各項支出、投資決策，進而形成拖累因素。另外，歐盟的「下
一代歐盟復甦計畫」(NextGenerationEU)已在 9 月底截止撥款申請，並將在年底前

三、日本—AI 投資續強，帶動機械訂單穩健復甦 
 8 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 5.2%，其中製造業訂單月增 1.6%，人
工智慧相關投資仍是主要驅動力，資通訊產業訂單月增 13.0%，有色金屬也大幅
反彈；汽車產業月增 13.1%，並已連續四個月成長，顯示投資需求穩健。非製造
業訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 2.9%，其中租賃業成長尤為強勁；運輸業、建築業在
7 月大幅成長後出現回落；零售業則在連續三個月下滑後月增 29.2%。整體而言，
機械訂單數據顯示企業資本支出呈現更廣泛且穩固的復甦趨勢，尤以 AI 相關投
資為主要支撐。 
四、中國—AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長 
9 月出口年增率較 8 月大幅增加 11.3 個百分點至 36.3%，出口總值為 4,472
億美元；累計前九個月出口年增率上升為 21.3%，累積出口總值為 33,707 億美元。
前九個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(108.1%)、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等，54.6%)、船舶(29.9%)、汽車(54.3%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備
出口加速成長，為 9 月出口年增率大幅上升的主要因素。 
9 月進口年增率較 8 月增 2.8 個百分點至 31%，進口總值為 3,137 億美元；累
計前九個月進口值成長 27.5%至 24,326 億美元。其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等)進口分別成長 63.5%、124.8%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部
分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前九個月貿易順差達
9,381 億美元，僅較去年同期增加 7.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時
伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價
值產品依賴國際供應鏈支持。 
9 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 8 月的 3.8%升至 5%，主因原油價格自 7 月起走
升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年增率
續揚 14.1 個百分點至 24.5%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至 21.6%，
此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升至
12.1%、12.3%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於近
期鋼鐵價格走弱，黑色金屬礦產開採、冶煉加工業出廠價格分別年減 3.4%、0.2%，

為主要拖累因素。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠價格年增率亦持續放慢。 
9 月消費者通膨(CPI)升 0.1 個百分點為 0.9%，但仍然是今年以來第三低的水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)持平於 1 %不變。該月燃油通膨續增 6.6 個百分點
至 14.9%，在客運成本增加之下，旅遊服務通膨亦增加 0.8 個百分點至 3.5%，此
外，由於手機價格上漲，通信工具通膨上揚 3.7 個百分點至 14.3%；其他商品及
服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏
力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府壓抑零售燃油及民生水電，故目前
原油價格高漲的影響尚未反映於消費端價格。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，
若油價維持高檔或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
展望未來，歐中談判已就汽車貿易達成部分共識，包含未來中國出口至歐盟
的混合動力汽車設定每年 35 萬輛的配額上限，且配額規模可逐年增加 6%。依照
歐盟預估 2026 年自中國進口約 80 萬輛混合動力汽車推算，中國對歐盟的混合動
力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。雖該協議不利於汽車出口，但有助
於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保護措施，中國車廠則可能會加速透過赴歐投資設
廠、提升當地生產比重等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市
占率。

面，受惠於 AI 伺服器與資料中心投資、科技產業供應鏈重組、美中貿易重組帶

國，但須留意地緣政治、全球貿易重組及 AI 投資循環變化，帶來的通膨及經濟
下行風險，建議各國政府提升農業生產效率及糧食安全，提升數位經濟及服務業
競爭力，強化財政紀律並提高公共投資效率，維持穩健貨幣政策與匯率穩定。

5.2%，以批發貿易受電子出口帶動表現尤佳。IMF 預期新加坡將持續受惠於 AI、

六、台灣—AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至 12.16%，
創 1988 年以來新高，主要受惠於 AI 技術快速發展帶動全球科技投資，半導體與
資通訊產品需求強勁，出口維持高速成長，同時民間投資與消費亦同步擴張，經
濟成長動能由內、外需共同支撐。另一方面，由於中東地緣政治風險升溫帶動油
價維持高檔，能源價格外溢效果推升整體物價，中經院將 2026 年 CPI 年增率預
估上修至 2.06%，惟整體通膨仍屬相對溫和。

3Q26 results miss from incremental production cost and higher opex: Silergy’s 
3Q26 GM of 47.4% and OPM of 13.3% missed MSe and consensus despite a better 
product mix (faster growth in AI and automotive). We attribute the miss to rising 
foundry/OSAT costs and higher AI datacenter R&D. Management guided opex to 
stay roughly flat in 1H27 and that it could be higher in 2H27 with new hires.
AI-related revenue and Gen4 process are key drivers for 2027 margin recovery: 
Management said 2027 revenue could grow at least 20-30% YoY, with AI-related 
revenue at c.25% and Gen4 at 50% of total revenue by end-2027. Management also 
guided 2027 GM to be >50% while continuing passing on cost to customers.
Automotive revenue could account for 20% of 2027 total revenue: Management 
expects strong automotive growth in 2027, with dollar content per high-end vehicle 
rising from US$220 in 2026 to US$270-300 in 2027.

Base case, residual income model. We assume a cost of equity of 9.5% (2.0% risk-free rate, 6%
risk premium, beta of 1.25), an intermediate growth rate of 13.5%, and a terminal growth rate
of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Gross margin expansion amid stabilized competition
n A surge in LED demand as pricing drops to the sweet spot
n Greater-than-expected benefits from China's support in local semiconductors

n Higher-than-expected dividend payout ratio
Risks to Downside
n Lower gross margin amid intensified competition
n Weaker-than-expected benefits from China's support in local semiconductors
n Lower-than-expected dividend payout ratio

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 214: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G는 +11.5%, ASP는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

## Document 268: Anthropic上市次日暴跌，盘中一度离破发仅差5美分，AI交易“买方罢工”

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic上市次日暴跌，盘中一度离破发仅差5美分，AI交易“买方罢工”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 股价周五盘中⼀度⼤跌 15% ⾄ 83.05 美元，距 83 美元的发⾏价仅剩 5 美分，最终
收跌 8.5% 。市场⼈⼠描述当前局⾯为 AI 交易的买⽅罢⼯。投资者观望情绪浓厚， Vega IS
表⽰，若股价接近甚⾄跌破发⾏价，将考虑在公开市场买⼊。 Tikehau Capital 表⽰，希望
等待 IPO 波动性消退后再考虑进⼀步介⼊。
摘要：
史上最⼤ IPO 的光环正在消退。 Anthropic 上市第⼆⽇股价急剧下滑，⼏乎抹去⾸⽇涨幅，映射
出更⼴泛 AI 与科技板块的抛售情绪，同时也为竞争对⼿ OpenAI 的未来上市之路埋下变数。
Anthropic 股价周五盘中⼀度⼤跌 15% ⾄ 83.05 美元，距 83 美元的发⾏价仅剩 5 美分，随后⼩幅
反弹，最终收跌 8.5% 。该股在上市⾸⽇曾短暂将公司市值推升⾄⾼于亚⻢逊。
与此同时，承销银⾏⾏使超额配售权（绿鞋机制），进⼀步扩⼤股票供给，使 Anthropic 此次 IPO
及联合私募合计融资规模升⾄ 1195 亿美元。

市场⼤环境同样构成压⼒。周五，抛售浪潮从亚洲蔓延⾄美国，纳斯达克 100 指数下跌 1.5% ，
标普 500 指数跌 0.5% ，芝加哥期权交易所波动率指数（ VIX ）飙升⾄ 29 以上 —— 远超企业通常视
为市场欢迎 IPO 的⽔位线。
Anthropic 此次上市的战略意义超出单⼀公司范畴。作为⾸家上市的前沿 AI 模型开发商，其市场
表现实质上是对投资者能否为 AI 底层技术本⾝（⽽⾮芯⽚、数据中⼼等基础设施）定价买单的⼀
次直接测试。
买盘观望， AI 热度遭遇 " 买⽅罢⼯ "
Forte Securities 的 Emmanuel Valavanis 将当前局⾯描述为 " 针对 Anthropic 乃⾄整个 AI 和科技
交易的买⽅罢⼯ " 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
他指出，许多客⼾在此次 IPO 配售中未能获得份额，在未持有仓位、也⽆指数跟踪压⼒的情况
下，这部分投资者宁愿在场外等待更⻓期的价格发现，也不愿贸然接盘。
这⼀动态揭⽰出 IPO ⾸⽇溢价背后的脆弱性：当核⼼买家已在发⾏价锁定筹码，⼆级市场缺乏增
量资⾦⼊场时，股价承压⼏乎是必然结果。
对于投资者⽽⾔，当前市场波动加剧了观望情绪。Vega IS 基⾦经理 Olivier David 表⽰，若股价
接近甚⾄跌破发⾏价，其团队将考虑在公开市场买⼊。
历史性 IPO ：融资规模与市场地位
此次上市创下多项纪录。 Anthropic 成为史上最⼤规模 IPO 的主⻆，同时也是⾸家登陆美股的前
沿 AI 模型开发商，超越了 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉创下的 866 亿美元纪录。
在承销层⾯，⾼盛、摩根⼠丹利及另外 20 家银⾏共计获得 5.5 亿美元费⽤，同样刷新历史纪录。

IPO 及联合私募合计融资中， IPO 部分为 935 亿美元，私募部分为 260 亿美元。按当前股价计算，
Anthropic 市值约为 1.5 万亿美元，位列全球上市公司前 15 位。公司七位联合创始⼈的合计财富升
⾄ 1660 亿美元。
包括 Anthropic 在内，美国市场今年⾸次公开募股总规模已达 2580 亿美元，超越 2021 年 1950 亿
美元的历史⾼点（均剔除特殊⽬的收购公司等⾦融⼯具）。
基本⾯⽀撑：企业客⼾驱动营收⾼速增⻓
股价波动之外， Anthropic 的基本⾯数据提供了部分⽀撑。
根据监管⽂件，截⾄ 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇，公司年化运营收⼊达 810 亿美元，较 9 ⽉底增加 100 亿美元。截
⾄ 9 ⽉底，年度贡献收⼊超过 10 万美元的企业客⼾数量超过 11900 家，⽽ 2025 年初这⼀数字仅约
为 1500 家。
公司旗下⾯向软件开发者的编程代理产品 Claude Code 在业内获得⼴泛应⽤，监管⽂件显⽰其⽤
⼾甚⾄涵盖 NASA ⼯程师。 Anthropic 以专注企业客⼾为核⼼策略，这⼀路径赢得了部分机构投

资者的认可。
Tikehau Capital 资本市场策略主管 Raphael Thuin 对此评价称， " 这家公司在短短数年内创造的财
富令⼈印象深刻，⾃由现⾦流消耗也相对合理 " ，但同时表⽰，其团队在 AI 领域更倾向于布局多
元化业务，且希望等待 IPO 后的波动性消退后再考虑进⼀步介⼊。

## Document 306: Do Italian households have what it takes to finance Europe’s next ideas_ (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Do Italian households have what it takes to finance Europe’s next ideas_ (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

18 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
DO ITALIAN HOUSEHOLDS HAVE WHAT IT TAKES TO FINANCE EUROPE’S NEXT IDEAS?
Andrea d’Aquino
Between 2019 and 2025, US value added grew at twice the pace of Europe’s, driven largely by technology and labour productivity 1,2. Yet Europe
does not lack saving to finance a similar development: over the past ten years, Euro area gross national saving exceeded domestic investment
by about €300 bn a year, equivalent to 15% of productive investment 3, and in 2024-2025 gross capital outflows to the United States averaged

€785 billion a year 4. The situation described therefore creates a “European paradox”, where household particularly save but do not take on so
much risk to foster innovation as in the US.
Safety versus risk: how Italian and European households differ
In Q2 2026, Euro area households’ gross financial wealth grew by 6.5% y/y to approximately €37 trillion, of which just over 30% is held in
currency and deposits (€11,165 billion), compared with about 10% in the United States. Italy has the lowest share of currency and deposits

among the major Euro area economies (see Chart 1). In Q2 2026, Italian households’ financial wealth grew by 6.3% y/y, in line with the
Euro area, reaching €6,609 billion, of which 24.2% is held in currency and deposits (about €1,598 billion), compared with 28.3% in France
(€2,151 billion), 32% in Spain (€1,168 billion) and 36% in Germany (€3,660 billion).
1 Fabio Panetta; Il risparmio nella società che cambia, Banca d’Italia; 30th October 2026.
2 Luisa Carpinelli et al.; The Great Divide: productivity dynamics in the United States and euro area after the pandemic; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 924, Banca d’Italia; April

2025.
3 Productive investments are gross fixed investments minus residential construction.
4 It includes the item ‘other investments’, mainly made up of deposits, loans, and trade receivables.
SOURCE: EUROSTAT, BNL ECONOMIC RESEARCH
In Italy the largest share of households’ total financial assets is invested in equity
and investment fund shares, less in currency and deposits

Italian risk is tied up mostly in unlisted firms
Judging by this metric, European households’ risk appetite is lower than that of their US counterparts. Another metric to look at: the listed
shares. They account for 5% of Euro area gross financial wealth 5 (3.1% in Italy, 3.7% in France, 6.6% in Spain, 7.2% in Germany). However,
once holdings in unlisted companies are included, the share of risk capital rises to 15% in the Euro area, 34% in the United States and

more than 20% in Italy.
A large part of the gap reflects the wider presence in Europe of unlisted firms, largely family-owned. In Italy in particular, unlisted shares
account for 17.1% of gross financial wealth, just over 7 pp above the Euro area average, compared with 12.4% in France, 7.7% in Spain and
2.6% in Germany (see Chart 2). Unlisted family firms are on average smaller and less productive than the system as a whole, especially
among medium-sized and large firms and in technology- and knowledge-intensive sectors 6.

Italian households’ risk capital is therefore largely held in forms that are hard to diversify and in firms far from the technological frontier,
while the risk capital reaching the youngest firms (less than 12 years old) is scarce, even though these firms are very active in innovation:
innovative start-ups account for just over 2% of young firms but hold about 44% of that category’s patents. Similarly, venture-capital-
backed firms are barely 0.2% of young firms but account for around 12% of total patents of the youngest firms 7.

Italian inheritances will largely pass on bricks
Investment choices are also linked to demographics: risk appetite tends to rise until adulthood and then declines with advancing age 8.
According to Banca d’Italia 9, in the coming years in Italy, those born between 1966 and 1975 will benefit from wealth transfers to a greater
extent than earlier cohorts: inheritances would account for around 40% of their wealth, compared with 30% for those born between 1951

and 1965. All else being equal, households expecting to receive an inheritance hold more financial assets. What will change hands is
mainly real assets, above all housing: in 2022 (latest available data), households whose reference person was born between 1941 and
1950 held on average around 62% of their total wealth in dwellings, 28% in financial assets and almost 10% in equity stakes in businesses.
As a result, once households receive the inheritance, their portfolios could become more tilted towards housing, which may lead to a

rebalancing towards financial assets.
The challenge for the Euro area in the coming years will be to press ahead with the creation of a single market for capital, in order to
provide greater investment opportunities and avert the “European savings paradox”.
andrea.aquino@bnpparibas.com
5 The US data is not available for listed share only.
6 Michele Cascarano et al.; Le imprese familiari e i passaggi generazionali in Italia; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1068, Banca d’Italia; October 2026.

7 Maria Giulia Cassinis et al.; Innovative firms unveiled: economic and financial insight from Italian start-ups; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 967, Banca d’Italia; October 2025.
8 Raffaella Giordano et al.; Population ageing and portfolio choices in the main EU countries; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1036, Banca d’Italia; July 2026.
9 Relazione annuale sul 2025, Banca d’Italia; May 2026.

## Document 322: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

available by WEG does not represent an offer from Westpac or any of its subsidiaries (other than

## Document 340: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

for a changing
world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM) (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note, 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 October that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available September data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5-2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five-year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI- and semiconductor-
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housing, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private-sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5 bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong 
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflation risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weakening 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa-City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI-electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re-export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger demand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low-income 
households. Despite the quarterly 
moderation, we maintain our full-year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass-through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

• Others: AUD corporates  
^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back 
gold amid USD strength. This is unlikely to persist 
Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk. While we remain constructive on the long-term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near-term risk-reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long-term AI growth opportunities. The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI-related beneficiaries. For investors, the 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk, and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem. 
Non-AI-related factors, including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk. This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50% to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses, although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI-related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversification. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long-term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives, and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.
